**Standing implementation workflow — shared mechanics first.** Before starting a skill, search existing class skills, runtime helpers and tests for matching mechanics. Reuse or extend those services, extracting a common helper when needed; add new machinery only for a documented behavioral difference. Check the shared consumers when changing them. Examples include Teeth/Multiple Shot projectile fans, Mind Blast/Conversion/Confuse allegiance and target handling, and Blade Sentinel/Miasma Chain movement. Shared infrastructure does not make distinct skill rules interchangeable. For temporary actors, check existing native timer ownership and reload hooks before adding another lifetime controller.

Each new skill checkpoint should identify the related skills/helpers inspected, what was reused, any behavior requiring a separate implementation, and the regression checks covering affected existing skills. Apply this review at the start of the work, before designing a new controller.

Latest approved Revive rule: retain whatever abilities the eligible monster had while alive, including its current native spells. Later global monster conversion updates the shared repertoire for both living and revived versions. No Revive-only spell whitelist or per-ability replacement gate is required; pending world spell conversion must not block learned Revive. Ownership, hostility, lifecycle and actual corpse eligibility remain separate requirements. See the [recorded decision](../tools/reference/revive-native-behavior-policy.json).

Replacing UO spells with D2 spells for **all UO monsters**, including wild versions, is a [separate game-wide task](monster-spell-conversion.md). Revive reuses the converted repertoire under its own eligibility/lifecycle rules. This scope decision adds no runtime handler and does not require blocking unrelated class skills on the whole world conversion.

The [native Revive replacement design](revive-behavior-replacements.md) keeps compatible movement/melee/presentation and separates conflicting breath damage and UO caster routines into explicit role adapters. Generic UO Heal/Greater Heal/Cure are addressed in the later global conversion; until then Revive retains existing monster abilities, including special healing/resurrection. Dragon/Lich classifications and replacement numbers remain unassigned. Summon Resist inheritance is a recorded historical source/documentation conflict, not an established current-build bonus. This is design progress only: no runtime change, new handler or new native acceptance is claimed.
